Yeah… I guess I’m here because I just haven’t been feeling right for a long time. Most days I wake up and it’s like there’s already this gray weight sitting on me before I even get out of bed. It’s not just a bad mood that passes. It’s more like this low, hollow feeling, like I’m there physically but I’m just going through the motions. I keep trying to push through because that’s what I’ve always done, but lately it feels heavy enough that I figured I probably needed to talk to somebody.

I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I know there are things I used to do, or things that were supposed to feel good, but I don’t know if I’ve sat down and measured it that way, you know?

Some days I’ll still do the same stuff because it’s part of life, or because other people are doing it, but whether I’m enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know. It’s kind of hard for me to separate “not enjoying it” from just being tired or distracted or whatever else is going on in my head. I guess I’d have to think more about what I used to like before all this and compare it, because right now it all sort of blends together.

I keep trying to push through because that’s what I’ve always done, but lately it feels heavy enough that I figured I probably needed to talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over three years now. About three years and three months, if I’m really thinking about it.\n\nI can kind of trace it back to when the groundskeeping contract changed our schedule. The longer days just took over everything, and after that it felt like I never really got back to myself. At first I thought I was just worn out and needed to get used to it, but then months kept passing, and then years, and it was still there. So yeah… around three years and some change.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I know there are things I used to do, or things that were supposed to feel good, but I don’t know if I’ve sat down and measured it that way, you know?\n\nSome days I’ll still do the same stuff because it’s part of life, or because other people are doing it, but whether I’m enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know. It’s kind of hard for me to separate “not enjoying it” from just being tired or distracted or whatever else is going on in my head. I guess I’d have to think more about what I used to like before all this and compare it, because right now it all sort of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know that sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I don’t mean to be. It’s just hard for me to tell what counts as “lost interest” versus me just not having the energy or not being able to get my head into things the way I used to.\n\nLike, there are things I still do because they’re there, because it’s routine, or because people expect me to show up for them. But if you asked me whether I’m sitting there thinking, “Yeah, I really enjoy this,” I don’t know. I don’t check in with myself that way much. Most of the time I’m just trying to get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… maybe? But I can’t say it clearly. It’s more like everything feels kind of muted, and I’m not sure if that means I lost interest or if I’m just worn down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m trying to think of something, and nothing really jumps out at me. Not in a clear way, anyway.

Like, I do things during the week, I go through the motions, and sometimes something is… fine, I guess. But if you mean actual pleasure, like I felt lighter or glad I was doing it, I’m not sure I can point to anything recent and say, “Yes, that was it.” I don’t really keep track of it, and when I look back, the days kind of run together.

There might be small moments where I’m not feeling as weighed down, but I don’t know if that counts as enjoyment. It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I can’t think of anything specific right now.

I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”

Sometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.

It’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.

Most of the time I’m just trying to get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… maybe? But I can’t say it clearly. It’s more like everything feels kind of muted, and I’m not sure if that means I lost interest or if I’m just worn down.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt pleasure or enjoyment from?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something, and nothing really jumps out at me. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nLike, I do things during the week, I go through the motions, and sometimes something is… fine, I guess. But if you mean actual pleasure, like I felt lighter or glad I was doing it, I’m not sure I can point to anything recent and say, “Yes, that was it.” I don’t really keep track of it, and when I look back, the days kind of run together.\n\nThere might be small moments where I’m not feeling as weighed down, but I don’t know if that counts as enjoyment. It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I can’t think of anything specific right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.

I’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.

It’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.

I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.

But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.

Some nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.

I guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?

But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. Do NOT force an unrelated item.\n- On your first reply of the consultation, always return the item whose key is your\n  chief_complaint_symptom_code, whatever the doctor opened with.\n- Do not answer the doctor, and do not write anything outside the JSON.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"key\": \"<key>\", \"name\": \"<name>\", \"description\": \"<description>\", \"reason\": \"<why this item>\"}\n"
  },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.

It’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.

And even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”

Maybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. But it doesn’t last. By midmorning, a lot of days, my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day, even if I’m just loading tools or getting things organized. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.

Then by the time I get home, I’m just dragging. And the frustrating part is, even if it’s Sunday and I sleep in, or I don’t have the usual workday, I still don’t really feel recharged. I can still drag through simple things like making dinner or picking up around the house. So yeah, I’d say it’s fairly consistent. It might get worse as the day goes on, but I don’t really get that clear “energized” part of the day.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’m here because I just haven’t been feeling right for a long time. Most days I wake up and it’s like there’s already this gray weight sitting on me before I even get out of bed. I go to work, I’m out there with the crew doing landscaping, trying to keep moving, and I’m around my kids too, but even then it’s like I can’t really shake it.\n\nIt’s not just a bad mood that passes. It’s more like this low, hollow feeling, like I’m there physically but I’m just going through the motions. I keep trying to push through because that’s what I’ve always done, but lately it feels heavy enough that I figured I probably needed to talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over three years now. About three years and three months, if I’m really thinking about it.\n\nI can kind of trace it back to when the groundskeeping contract changed our schedule. The longer days just took over everything, and after that it felt like I never really got back to myself. At first I thought I was just worn out and needed to get used to it, but then months kept passing, and then years, and it was still there. So yeah… around three years and some change.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I know there are things I used to do, or things that were supposed to feel good, but I don’t know if I’ve sat down and measured it that way, you know?\n\nSome days I’ll still do the same stuff because it’s part of life, or because other people are doing it, but whether I’m enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know. It’s kind of hard for me to separate “not enjoying it” from just being tired or distracted or whatever else is going on in my head. I guess I’d have to think more about what I used to like before all this and compare it, because right now it all sort of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know that sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I don’t mean to be. It’s just hard for me to tell what counts as “lost interest” versus me just not having the energy or not being able to get my head into things the way I used to.\n\nLike, there are things I still do because they’re there, because it’s routine, or because people expect me to show up for them. But if you asked me whether I’m sitting there thinking, “Yeah, I really enjoy this,” I don’t know. I don’t check in with myself that way much. Most of the time I’m just trying to get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… maybe? But I can’t say it clearly. It’s more like everything feels kind of muted, and I’m not sure if that means I lost interest or if I’m just worn down.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt pleasure or enjoyment from?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something, and nothing really jumps out at me. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nLike, I do things during the week, I go through the motions, and sometimes something is… fine, I guess. But if you mean actual pleasure, like I felt lighter or glad I was doing it, I’m not sure I can point to anything recent and say, “Yes, that was it.” I don’t really keep track of it, and when I look back, the days kind of run together.\n\nThere might be small moments where I’m not feeling as weighed down, but I don’t know if that counts as enjoyment. It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I can’t think of anything specific right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nThen by the time I get home, I’m just dragging. I can still drag through simple things like making dinner or picking up around the house. So yeah, I’d say it’s fairly consistent. I don’t know… that’s a harder one to answer.

I mean, I can think of things I wish I’d handled better, sure. I think everybody can. I’ll look back on a conversation or something I didn’t get done and kind of chew on it more than I probably need to. But “worthless” feels like a big word, and I’m not sure I’d say it exactly like that.

It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly.

I mean, I have days where I’ll be looking at something and realize I’m not really taking it in, but I don’t know if that’s concentration exactly or just… I don’t know, being worn down or distracted. Like if I’m reading through something or trying to figure out the order of what needs doing, sometimes I have to go back over it, but I don’t know that I’d say I have a major problem focusing.

At work, I usually still get through what I need to get through. I might be slower about it, or I’ll kind of stand there for a second trying to get myself organized, but I haven’t really thought of it as “I can’t concentrate.” It’s more like everything takes a little more pushing than it should.

So… maybe some trouble, but I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve paid as much attention to as the tiredness.

I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. But it doesn’t last. By midmorning, a lot of days, my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day, even if I’m just loading tools or getting things organized. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nThen by the time I get home, I’m just dragging. And the frustrating part is, even if it’s Sunday and I sleep in, or I don’t have the usual workday, I still don’t really feel recharged. I can still drag through simple things like making dinner or picking up around the house. So yeah, I’d say it’s fairly consistent. It might get worse as the day goes on, but I don’t really get that clear “energized” part of the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s a harder one to answer.\n\nI mean, I can think of things I wish I’d handled better, sure. I think everybody can. I’ll look back on a conversation or something I didn’t get done and kind of chew on it more than I probably need to. But “worthless” feels like a big word, and I’m not sure I’d say it exactly like that.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s often in the way you mean. It’s more like I get frustrated with myself, like I should be doing better or keeping up better, and then I go around in circles about it. But I’m not sure if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes too strongly and make it sound like something it isn’t, but I also wouldn’t say I never feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or focusing on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly.\n\nI mean, I have days where I’ll be looking at something and realize I’m not really taking it in, but I don’t know if that’s concentration exactly or just… I don’t know, being worn down or distracted. Like if I’m reading through something or trying to figure out the order of what needs doing, sometimes I have to go back over it, but I don’t know that I’d say I have a major problem focusing.\n\nAt work, I usually still get through what I need to get through. I might be slower about it, or I’ll kind of stand there for a second trying to get myself organized, but I haven’t really thought of it as “I can’t concentrate.” It’s more like everything takes a little more pushing than it should.\n\nSo… maybe some trouble, but I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve paid as much attention to as the tiredness.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. But “worthless” feels like a big word, and I’m not sure I’d say it exactly like that.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s often in the way you mean. It’s more like I get frustrated with myself, like I should be doing better or keeping up better, and then I go around in circles about it. But I’m not sure if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes too strongly and make it sound like something it isn’t, but I also wouldn’t say I never feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or focusing on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly.\n\nI mean, I have days where I’ll be looking at something and realize I’m not really taking it in, but I don’t know if that’s concentration exactly or just… I don’t know, being worn down or distracted. Like if I’m reading through something or trying to figure out the order of what needs doing, sometimes I have to go back over it, but I don’t know that I’d say I have a major problem focusing.\n\nAt work, I usually still get through what I need to get through. I might be slower about it, or I’ll kind of stand there for a second trying to get myself organized, but I haven’t really thought of it as “I can’t concentrate.” It’s more like everything takes a little more pushing than it should.\n\nSo… maybe some trouble, but I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve paid as much attention to as the tiredness.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 48 / landscaping_or_groundskeeping_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t noticed a big, clear change where I could say, “Yeah, my appetite is definitely different now.”

Some days I don’t feel that interested in food, but I don’t know if that’s appetite changing or just me not wanting to deal with making something. Other days I eat normally enough, especially if it’s just routine — lunch at work, dinner if it’s there. I haven’t really been tracking it or anything.

Over the past three years, if there’s been a change, it’s not something that jumped out at me. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some major increase or loss of appetite that I’m aware of. It’s more like… food is just one of those things I get through, I guess, but I can’t point to a definite change.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F32.4, F32.8, F32.9, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3, F33.4, F33.5, F33.8, F33.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I’m here because I just haven’t been feeling right for a long time. Most days I wake up and it’s like there’s already this gray weight sitting on me before I even get out of bed. I go to work, I’m out there with the crew doing landscaping, trying to keep moving, and I’m around my kids too, but even then it’s like I can’t really shake it.\n\nIt’s not just a bad mood that passes. It’s more like this low, hollow feeling, like I’m there physically but I’m just going through the motions. I keep trying to push through because that’s what I’ve always done, but lately it feels heavy enough that I figured I probably needed to talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over three years now. About three years and three months, if I’m really thinking about it.\n\nI can kind of trace it back to when the groundskeeping contract changed our schedule. The longer days just took over everything, and after that it felt like I never really got back to myself. At first I thought I was just worn out and needed to get used to it, but then months kept passing, and then years, and it was still there. So yeah… around three years and some change.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I know there are things I used to do, or things that were supposed to feel good, but I don’t know if I’ve sat down and measured it that way, you know?\n\nSome days I’ll still do the same stuff because it’s part of life, or because other people are doing it, but whether I’m enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know. It’s kind of hard for me to separate “not enjoying it” from just being tired or distracted or whatever else is going on in my head. I guess I’d have to think more about what I used to like before all this and compare it, because right now it all sort of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know that sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I don’t mean to be. It’s just hard for me to tell what counts as “lost interest” versus me just not having the energy or not being able to get my head into things the way I used to.\n\nLike, there are things I still do because they’re there, because it’s routine, or because people expect me to show up for them. But if you asked me whether I’m sitting there thinking, “Yeah, I really enjoy this,” I don’t know. I don’t check in with myself that way much. Most of the time I’m just trying to get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… maybe? But I can’t say it clearly. It’s more like everything feels kind of muted, and I’m not sure if that means I lost interest or if I’m just worn down.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt pleasure or enjoyment from?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something, and nothing really jumps out at me. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nLike, I do things during the week, I go through the motions, and sometimes something is… fine, I guess. But if you mean actual pleasure, like I felt lighter or glad I was doing it, I’m not sure I can point to anything recent and say, “Yes, that was it.” I don’t really keep track of it, and when I look back, the days kind of run together.\n\nThere might be small moments where I’m not feeling as weighed down, but I don’t know if that counts as enjoyment. It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I can’t think of anything specific right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. But it doesn’t last. By midmorning, a lot of days, my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day, even if I’m just loading tools or getting things organized. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nThen by the time I get home, I’m just dragging. And the frustrating part is, even if it’s Sunday and I sleep in, or I don’t have the usual workday, I still don’t really feel recharged. I can still drag through simple things like making dinner or picking up around the house. So yeah, I’d say it’s fairly consistent. It might get worse as the day goes on, but I don’t really get that clear “energized” part of the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s a harder one to answer.\n\nI mean, I can think of things I wish I’d handled better, sure. I’m not sure I’ve noticed that in a clear way.

I mean, I don’t think anyone’s told me I’m talking slower or moving strangely slow, if that’s what you mean. With my kind of work, I’m moving around a lot anyway, so it’s hard to judge. I go to work, I’m out there with the crew doing landscaping, trying to keep moving, and I’m around my kids too, but even then it’s like I can’t really shake it.\n\nIt’s not just a bad mood that passes. It’s more like this low, hollow feeling, like I’m there physically but I’m just going through the motions. I keep trying to push through because that’s what I’ve always done, but lately it feels heavy enough that I figured I probably needed to talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over three years now. About three years and three months, if I’m really thinking about it.\n\nI can kind of trace it back to when the groundskeeping contract changed our schedule. The longer days just took over everything, and after that it felt like I never really got back to myself. At first I thought I was just worn out and needed to get used to it, but then months kept passing, and then years, and it was still there. So yeah… around three years and some change.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying activities that you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean… I know there are things I used to do, or things that were supposed to feel good, but I don’t know if I’ve sat down and measured it that way, you know?\n\nSome days I’ll still do the same stuff because it’s part of life, or because other people are doing it, but whether I’m enjoying it the way I used to… I don’t know. It’s kind of hard for me to separate “not enjoying it” from just being tired or distracted or whatever else is going on in my head. I guess I’d have to think more about what I used to like before all this and compare it, because right now it all sort of blends together.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m honestly not sure. I know that sounds like I’m dodging the question, but I don’t mean to be. It’s just hard for me to tell what counts as “lost interest” versus me just not having the energy or not being able to get my head into things the way I used to.\n\nLike, there are things I still do because they’re there, because it’s routine, or because people expect me to show up for them. But if you asked me whether I’m sitting there thinking, “Yeah, I really enjoy this,” I don’t know. I don’t check in with myself that way much. Most of the time I’m just trying to get through the day and do what I’m supposed to do.\n\nSo… maybe? But I can’t say it clearly. It’s more like everything feels kind of muted, and I’m not sure if that means I lost interest or if I’m just worn down.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that you've done and actually felt pleasure or enjoyment from?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of something, and nothing really jumps out at me. Not in a clear way, anyway.\n\nLike, I do things during the week, I go through the motions, and sometimes something is… fine, I guess. But if you mean actual pleasure, like I felt lighter or glad I was doing it, I’m not sure I can point to anything recent and say, “Yes, that was it.” I don’t really keep track of it, and when I look back, the days kind of run together.\n\nThere might be small moments where I’m not feeling as weighed down, but I don’t know if that counts as enjoyment. It’s more like, “Okay, this isn’t terrible,” not really something I’d call pleasure. So… I don’t know. I can’t think of anything specific right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know how to answer that exactly. I mean, that’s a heavy question, and I’m not trying to be difficult. I just don’t know if what’s gone through my head is the same as what you mean by “thought about ending my life.”\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn down, I’ll have thoughts like I don’t want to keep doing everything, or I wish I could just not have to deal with anything for a while. But I don’t know if that’s the same thing as actually thinking about ending my life. I don’t really have a clean answer where I can say yes or no and feel like I’m being accurate.\n\nIt’s more like… I get overwhelmed, and my mind goes to dark places, but I’m not sure how to describe it without making it sound more definite than it is. I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. I can’t honestly sit here and say, “Yes, I’ve had that exact thought,” because I don’t know that I have in a way I could point to. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But whether that crosses into actually thinking, “I’m going to take my own life,” I don’t know. I don’t have a clear memory of planning it out or deciding something like that. It’s vague and scary and kind of hard to talk about.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not a significant amount, at least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nMy weight goes up and down a little, I guess, like depending on how much I’m working or what I’m eating, but I haven’t had one of those situations where my clothes are suddenly hanging off me or people are commenting on it. I also don’t weigh myself very consistently, so I can’t give you an exact number over three years.\n\nSo, I’d say no major weight loss that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with your sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if I’d call it “problems” exactly without knowing what you mean.\n\nSome nights are worse than others, and I don’t always feel like my sleep is doing what it’s supposed to do, if that makes sense. But I haven’t really kept track of it or anything. I’m not the kind of person who writes down when I go to bed or how many times I wake up, so I’d be guessing a little.\n\nI guess I’d say… maybe, but I’m not sure how much of it counts as a sleep problem versus just regular life stuff or being tired from work. What kind of sleep problems are you asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. That’s probably one of the bigger things, honestly.\n\nIt’s not just “I’m tired after work,” because I know landscaping is physical and I’m not twenty-five anymore. I expect to be worn out at the end of the day. But this is more like… by midmorning, sometimes, my body already feels like I’ve put in a full day. Like I’ll be loading tools or getting equipment together, and it feels heavier than it should, or I’m looking at the job plan and even that feels like it takes more out of me than it ought to.\n\nAnd even on a Sunday, if I sleep in or try to catch up, I don’t really bounce back. I’ll still kind of drag through basic stuff—making dinner, putting things away, just getting myself moving. It’s like there’s not much in the tank to start with, and then the day barely has to begin before I’m already running low.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel more energized at any point in the day, or is the tiredness consistent from morning to night?\n\nPatient: It’s pretty much most of the day, honestly. I don’t really have a part of the day where I’d say, “Oh, now I feel good, now I’ve got my energy back.”\n\nMaybe right when I first get up I can tell myself, okay, let’s get moving, maybe today won’t be so bad. But it doesn’t last. By midmorning, a lot of days, my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day, even if I’m just loading tools or getting things organized. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nThen by the time I get home, I’m just dragging. And the frustrating part is, even if it’s Sunday and I sleep in, or I don’t have the usual workday, I still don’t really feel recharged. I can still drag through simple things like making dinner or picking up around the house. So yeah, I’d say it’s fairly consistent. It might get worse as the day goes on, but I don’t really get that clear “energized” part of the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or worthless about things you've done or haven't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… that’s a harder one to answer.\n\nI mean, I can think of things I wish I’d handled better, sure. I think everybody can. I’ll look back on a conversation or something I didn’t get done and kind of chew on it more than I probably need to. But “worthless” feels like a big word, and I’m not sure I’d say it exactly like that.\n\nGuilty… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if it’s often in the way you mean. It’s more like I get frustrated with myself, like I should be doing better or keeping up better, and then I go around in circles about it. But I’m not sure if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nSo I guess I’d say I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t want to say yes too strongly and make it sound like something it isn’t, but I also wouldn’t say I never feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble concentrating or focusing on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one clearly.\n\nI mean, I have days where I’ll be looking at something and realize I’m not really taking it in, but I don’t know if that’s concentration exactly or just… I don’t know, being worn down or distracted. Like if I’m reading through something or trying to figure out the order of what needs doing, sometimes I have to go back over it, but I don’t know that I’d say I have a major problem focusing.\n\nAt work, I usually still get through what I need to get through. I might be slower about it, or I’ll kind of stand there for a second trying to get myself organized, but I haven’t really thought of it as “I can’t concentrate.” It’s more like everything takes a little more pushing than it should.\n\nSo… maybe some trouble, but I’m not sure. It’s not something I’ve paid as much attention to as the tiredness.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t noticed a big, clear change where I could say, “Yeah, my appetite is definitely different now.”\n\nSome days I don’t feel that interested in food, but I don’t know if that’s appetite changing or just me not wanting to deal with making something. Other days I eat normally enough, especially if it’s just routine — lunch at work, dinner if it’s there. I haven’t really been tracking it or anything.\n\nOver the past three years, if there’s been a change, it’s not something that jumped out at me. I wouldn’t say I’ve had some major increase or loss of appetite that I’m aware of. It’s more like… food is just one of those things I get through, I guess, but I can’t point to a definite change.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than you used to, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that in a clear way.\n\nI mean, I don’t think anyone’s told me I’m talking slower or moving strangely slow, if that’s what you mean. With my kind of work, I’m moving around a lot anyway, so it’s hard to judge. There are times I probably feel like I’m dragging a bit, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing as moving slower than I used to.\n\nAs far as being restless, I don’t think I’m pacing around or unable to sit still. I might shift around or have trouble getting comfortable sometimes, but I wouldn’t say that’s a big thing I’ve noticed. It’s more that I’m just kind of getting through the day, not really thinking, “Oh, I’m slowed down,” or “I can’t sit still.”\n\nSo I guess my answer is… I don’t really know. Nothing obvious that I can point to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would end your life if you decided to do it?\n\nPatient: I don’t really have a clear answer to that. I mean… I’ve never sat down and worked out some specific way, if that’s what you’re asking. It’s more that when you ask it like that, I kind of freeze up because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nI can’t point to a particular method or anything I’ve planned out. I haven’t had something where I was like, “This is exactly how I’d do it.” It’s not something I’ve mapped out in my head like that.\n\nSo… no, not in a specific way that I could describe.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

---------- turn 17 | patient:alignment | patient.build_alignment_messages() | model=gpt-5.5 ----------
========== INPUT [patient] ==========
[

I guess… like I was trying to say, I haven’t really gotten that far with it in my head. I know that sounds maybe evasive, but I don’t mean it that way.

When you say “how I would carry it out,” I think of having steps or some kind of plan, and I don’t have that. So no, I don’t have a way I’ve planned to carry it out. I can’t give you details because I really haven’t worked out details.

I guess I’d need you to explain what you mean a little more, because I don’t want to say the wrong thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts of actually taking your own life, not just wishing you didn't have to deal with things?\n\nPatient: I’m… I’m not sure. I know you’re trying to separate it out, and I get why, but even with you saying it that way, I still have a hard time knowing how to answer without making it sound like something very specific.\n\nI’ve had dark thoughts, yeah, and I’ve had moments where things feel so heavy that my mind goes somewhere ugly. But when you say “actually taking my own life,” like forming that thought in a clear way, I don’t know. But I also don’t want to say “no” too quickly, because then it feels like I’m brushing off how bad it can get in my head sometimes.\n\nIt’s more like… I’ll get to a place where I just want everything to stop, or I don’t want to be in my own life for a while, if that makes sense. But it doesn’t last. By midmorning, a lot of days, my body feels like I’ve already put in a full day, even if I’m just loading tools or getting things organized. And it’s not only the physical stuff either—checking the job plan, figuring out what needs to happen first, even that can feel like it takes more effort than it should.\n\nThen by the time I get home, I’m just dragging.
